Two of my other claims were also overstated: its 1R is **not** "inside the noise" (min **12.8× the spread**, 5th pct 61.6×, i.e. tight but placeable), and stripping the top of an asymmetric payoff removes the MECHANISM — a caveat this very ledger already records for the RobBooker battery. | **THE REJECTION SURVIVES, but on a different and independent test** (`scratchpad/tv_ger40_probe.py`): **the edge lives in the bracket's ANCHOR, not in the entry.** The script sets `entryPrice := high` and targets `high + 3×(high − min(low[1],low[2]))` while the fill is the next open, so the risk actually taken and the "3R" target are measured from different anchors — the advertised 1:3 is not what the code does, and on bars where the close sits on the recent low the realised R explodes (the +24.7 R trade; 1R spans ~20×, min 0.05× ATR). Re-run with **the same published entry and a self-consistent bracket** (risk from the fill, target 3× that risk): published-as-is **t=1.37**, floored at 0.25/0.5/1.0×ATR **t=1.70/1.86/1.65**, pure 1.0×ATR 3:1 **t=1.49** — **never t>2 in any specification**, and R/yr falls from the headline +8.6 to +2.8…+3.7. Cross-asset, the same entry with a stable bracket over 19 assets gives **median t +0.63, 0 above t=2** (best XAGUSD 1.98). So the entry is ordinary and the headline was risk-unit instability, not edge. | **STANDING CHECK ADDED**: the engine's applicability gate tests the MEDIAN stop against ATR and therefore cannot catch per-trade degeneracy — any candidate whose stop is a recent extreme anchored differently from the fill must be **re-run with a self-consistent bracket** before its R-based numbers are believed. LESSON about my own process: "the result is outlier-driven" is a *hypothesis about the cause*, not a verdict — it has to be checked against what the live book already tolerates, and then confirmed by an independent test that does not share the same failure mode.

## [2026-08-03] research | DECOUPLING THE ENTRY FROM THE BRACKET — the user's hypothesis tested at scale, and a new standing method. User: *"la plupart ne sont jamais retenus car ils ne passent pas les critères SL/TP … re-analyse littéralement tout … différentes variantes de combinaisons SL/TP (SL ATR de la timeframe d'entrée) … beaucoup de candidats pour remplacer ma brique 2 sur l'or."* **THE PREMISE IS CORRECT AND IT IS A REAL UNLOCK.** Every earlier corpus pass tested strategies *at their published bracket*, which conflates "is the entry informative" with "did the author pick a workable stop" — and this ledger is full of rejections that are unambiguously the latter (point-denominated stops worth $0.30 on BTC, mid-line trailing whipsaw, targets on a moving average, and **1150 of 5759 Pine scripts discarded solely for lacking a hard SL+TP**). New `tv_transpile.compile_script(require_bracket=False)` compiles the ENTRY ONLY while keeping every other filter structural (no `request.*`/`security()`, no grid/DCA, no `pyramiding>=2`, no `lookahead_on`, no traded volume): **distinct compiled rules 375 → 1405**, regression-checked (bracket mode still yields exactly 375). Unified signal library = PINE 5759 + 21 MQL4 ports + MQL5 ports + a ~210-rule parameterised classical canon → **988 rules on XAUUSD D1**, deduplicated on the **signal array** rather than the file (Donchian-20 appears 8× under different titles). Swept **161 ATR brackets** (SL 0.5–3×ATR *of the entry timeframe*, TP 0.5–6×SL, 3 time caps) × 3 direction variants = **350,018 cells** in 1.4 min, on a new vectorised engine verified **32/32 exact** against `mt4_backtest.simulate` (identical trade counts, exit bars, max |ΔR| = 0.0 — the first run failed 32/32 and the cause was a mis-shifted ATR *in the verification harness*, which is precisely why the check exists). | **THREE NESTED NEGATIVES.** (1) **The raw arithmetic is a mirage.** 9.4× more t>2 cells than chance, 39.8× at t>2.9 — but **long-only cells have median t +1.72 and short-only −1.75 before any rule is consulted**. Gold drifted; a long-biased ATR bracket collects it whatever bar it enters on. Scored against a **matched random-entry null** (same bracket, same direction mix, same raw signal count, same one-position sequencing; 8050 grid points × 400 draws), the **median t excess across 300k cells is +0.01**, and long-only rules are on average **worse than random long entries** (−0.16). This is the geometry baseline reproduced at scale instead of from 400 draws. (2) **The funnel is a candidate generator.** Plateau-ranked screening (median over all 161 brackets — no selection) yields **68 candidates**; **56** clear a full battery (recent-half null, bracket neighbourhood, cost +10 pts/side, outlier dependence, |corr| ≤ 0.06 to all four bricks, RoMaD ≥ gold's 0.73, book improvement at equal prop risk). Then the **PLACEBO PIPELINE** — every rule replaced by a random rule matched on signal count and long fraction, pushed through the identical code path, 3 replicates — yields **54.3 candidates (range 47–62), ratio 1.25×**, and the placebo **beats** the real corpus at every *statistical* stage (0.77–0.86×); the corpus only edges ahead at the two *economic* filters. (3) **The discriminating tests don't discriminate.** Walk-forward + one-bar fill delay: REAL 36%/21% vs PLACEBO **38%/26%**. Two leaks were found and fixed in my own tests: the split-half/walk-forward nulls were drawn from the **whole sample** while t was measured on **one half** (`gold_lib.null_ts` now takes `lo`/`hi`; correcting it moved the placebo *against* the strategies, 33%→38%), and the candidate list itself was built on the whole sample so only the *bracket* was ever out of sample. A **leak-free TRUE walk-forward** (rule AND bracket chosen on first-half bars only, null drawn from second-half bars) gives **REAL median OOS excess +0.05 vs PLACEBO +0.41**, with 2 of 20 real cells at p<0.05 where chance gives 1.0 — and those two are the same rule under two names (`donchian55 long` = "Turtle Trend Trading System"). **0 validated candidates.** | **BRICK 2's OWN BRACKET WAS NEVER THE CONSTRAINT.** `gold_brick2.py` reproduces its exact entry and runs **1728 brackets** over it, each against its own null (`tp_atr` had sat in `TurnOfMonthParams` defaulted to `None`, never swept). As deployed (SL 1.5×ATR, no TP, ~3-bar hold): n=92, +2.40 R/yr, **t=2.17 against a matched null of +1.47 → p_null = 0.217**; the seasonality signal contributes ~+0.70 of t, not significant. Across the grid **only 12 of 1728 points (1%) beat their own null — below the 5% chance rate**; best null-beating point +5.98 R/yr at p=0.050 (best-of-1728); max R/yr anywhere +19.2 but at p=0.057 (drift held 30 bars). Brick 2 **stands unchanged** on the grounds already recorded — pre-registered, live-verified 124/126, kept for decorrelation not return — but the hope that a different SL/TP makes it a 7–8 R/yr sleeve is removed. | **BEST CASE STUDY OF THE PREMISE:** `SilverStream v2` (EMA30>EMA50 + close>high[1]) ships a fixed **$2 TP / $1 SL**, which on gold at $2000–3400/oz is **0.03×ATR** — inside the spread. The earlier TradingView pass *had* this script and the applicability gate marked it **N/A: stop out of scale**, so it was never scored; re-bracketed at SL 1.5×ATR it was the strongest cell in the sweep (t=4.97, +7.9 R/yr, and its short leg makes ~0 where random shorts lose t=−2.2) — and it still dies in the placebo. The premise was right; the conclusion is unchanged. **NEW STANDING METHOD: every screening funnel this project runs must be re-run on matched random signals and its candidate count divided through.** 68 vs 54 is the difference between "we found 68 candidates" and "we found nothing". NOT DONE: the H1 timeframe (1106-rule library built, sweep stopped at 10/161 — it was starving the other runs, and the ledger already measures H1 as below chance while D1 is not). Tools: `RESEARCH_LOG_GOLD2.md`, scratchpad/gold_{lib,verify,signals,bsweep,null,cand,battery,run_battery,finalists,brick2,placebo,placebo_wf,truewf}.py.

## [2026-08-04] audit | **Brick 1 verified CORRECT; the silence has a different cause — and a second bug was found.** User: *"peux tu verifier si la brique 1 est bien codée en live car je n'ai pas pris de trade depuis 3-4 jours"*. | (1) **BRICK 1 IS SOUND.** `verify_brick1` = **830/830 exact match** (direction, entry price, R distance) between `live.signals.nas_orb_scan` and the frozen `run_atr_breakout`. | (2) **THE SILENCE IS EXPECTED FOR BRICK 1.** Replaying the live driver's decision on FRESH Pepperstone bars (`scratchpad/brick1_livecheck.py` — same `Broker`, same `to_true_utc` clock) shows the **low-vol regime gate has been CLOSED every session since 2026-07-28**: atr3/atr20 = 1.037, 1.007, 1.137, 1.304, 1.182, 1.103 against a gate of **<1.00**. Brick 1 is *designed* to stand down in a high-vol tape — the last two entries were 2026-07-24 and 2026-07-27, both at ratio ~0.94-1.00, i.e. right at the edge before the tape expanded. Calibration from its own history (`scratchpad/brick1_gaps.py`): it trades **37.6% of session days** (830 trades / 2208 days), median gap 1 day, **P(gap ≥ 4 days) = 16.6%** — 138 droughts in 8.6 yrs = **16/yr** — p95 = 10 days, max 31. A 4-day drought is unremarkable. | (3) **THE REAL CAUSE: THE RUNNER IS DOWN.** `edgelab/live/_out/runner.log` last wrote **2026-07-31 16:54** and holds nothing but its 10 startup lines — no `pre-session alert sent` (daily 08:30 ET) and no `daily Discord report sent` (daily 17:00 ET), both INFO to that same file — and no runner process exists on the machine. MT5 confirms it independently: **zero deals since the brick-2 gold entry of 2026-07-31 01:01 server**, and magics **101 (brick 1) and 105 (brick 4) have never traded at all** on this account. So the 3-4 silent days are ~4 days of runner downtime, not a signal failure. Per the config's own design (*"its ABSENCE is the alert"*) the missing Discord heartbeat was the intended tell — it was not acted on, which is the operational lesson. | (4) **NEW BUG FOUND — brick 2's live TIME-EXIT can never fire.** `signals._business_tom_flags` anchors `next_first` to **`day`'s own month-end** (`bmonth_end + BDay(1)`), so for any day in the first days of month M+1 it computes the window of **M+1 instead of M**. Measured over 2018-2026: `is_entry_day` True **108** times, **`is_exit_day` True 0 times**, `in_window` 108 instead of ~324 — `is_exit_day` is **structurally always False**. `GoldTomStrategy` passes neither `time_exit_at` nor `bars_held_limit` to `place_market`, so `is_exit_day` is its **only** exit path: the position rides to its broker-side stop instead of closing at the window end. Risk stays bounded (the 1.5×ATR SL is attached at the broker), the R distribution does not — the backtest's `time_exit` outcomes become stop-or-nothing. Concretely: the **open XAUUSD from 2026-07-31 should close at today's daily close** (backtest exit = close of the 2nd trading day of the next month = **2026-08-04**) **and will not**. It slipped through because `verify_brick2` only asserts **entry** days (the 124/126 headline) and never replays an exit — unlike bricks 3 and 4, whose cadence *and* exits are both replayed trade-for-trade. **LESSON: a parity harness that only checks entries certifies half a brick.** Tools: scratchpad/brick1_gaps.py, brick1_livecheck.py.

## [2026-08-04] correction + fix | **⚠️ MY "THE RUNNER IS DOWN" CLAIM WAS WRONG — the runner is on the VPS** (user: *"le runner est sur un vps actuellement"*). I read `edgelab/live/_out/runner.log`, which is the log of a runner started **on this machine** on 07-31 and stopped shortly after; the VPS keeps its own git clone and its own `_out/`, invisible from here. And the "zero deals since 07-31" I offered as independent corroboration **corroborates nothing** — every brick was correctly silent: brick 1 gated off by the low-vol filter every session since 07-28, brick 3 holding at 6/30 bars, brick 4 with no IBS signal, and brick 2 unable to exit *because of the bug below*. A perfectly healthy runner produces exactly that trace. LESSON: "no output" is evidence of downtime only where output was DUE; I inverted that, and the local log was never authority over a remote process. | **BRICK 2's TIME-EXIT FIXED**, user: *"fais les modif pour que la brique 2 sorte a time le 2eme jour du mois"*. Three things had to be right, and the second and third were only found by measuring. (1) **The anchor**: `_business_tom_flags` now anchors BOTH legs on `day`'s own month — the first `first_days` business days of the current month are the tail of the *previous* turn, not a window in the next month. (2) **The cadence**: the backtest exits at the **close** of the `first_days`-th trading day, while the driver wakes at the daily **rollover** and can only get the OPEN of the day it wakes on. On a ~24h instrument the rollover of day X+1 *is* the close of day X, so the trigger has to fire the day AFTER the exit day. Measured over 126 monthly trades (`scratchpad/brick2_exitday.py`): firing on the exit day itself costs **−6.41 R** of tracking error (+18.0 vs the backtest's +24.4 — a quarter of the edge), firing the next day **−0.33 R** (sd 0.062 R/trade). The 13% of exits sitting across a weekend gap cost +0.04 R on average, i.e. nothing. (3) **The calendar was the wrong instrument entirely**: a business-day version of (2) still lost **−5.62 R**, and `scratchpad/brick2_diag.py` located it exactly — **11 of 126 trades exit one bar early, every one of them a New Year or Easter month**, where a market holiday falls on a business day so the "3rd business day" is really only the 2nd *trading* day. The exit is backward-looking and therefore needs no calendar at all: `tom_state` now **counts the completed D1 bars of the current month** and closes once `first_days` of them are done. This also forced `GoldTomStrategy` onto `get_bars_raw` — `get_bars`' true-UTC index stamps a session at the *previous* calendar day, which would miscount the month whenever the 1st is a trading day. ENTRY stays on the business calendar because it is unavoidably forward-looking; its failure mode is benign (market shut on a Good Friday month-end → `MarketClosed` → month skipped, not entered late) and is the known 124/126. | **`verify_brick2` NOW REPLAYS TRADES, NOT JUST ENTRY DAYS** — the gap that let this ship. It runs the driver's real rollover cadence with the broker-side stop and compares whole trades, **like-for-like** (the 2 months live cannot enter are excluded rather than charged to the exit logic, which would have hidden the result): live **124 trades, 107 time-exits** (was **0**) vs 126 backtest, **+21.47 R vs +21.80 R on the comparable months = −0.33 R over 10.6 years**. Gate: at least one time-exit, nothing left unexited, |ΔR| ≤ 1. **verify 4/4 PASS**, bricks 1/3/4 byte-identical (830/830, cadence 0 re-opens, 314/314). Confirmed against the live terminal: raw D1 dates are trading dates, and the open XAUUSD from 07-31 now exits at the **rollover of Wed 2026-08-05 = the close of Tue 08-04**, the 2nd trading day, exactly as the backtest. ⚠️ `pytest` is not installed on this machine so the 22-test suite could NOT be run (it covers engine/propfirm/trade_rules, none of which this touches). **NOT YET LIVE: the VPS auto-updates from `origin/main`, so this needs a commit + push to take effect.** `edgelab/live/{signals,strategies,verify}.py`, scratchpad/brick2_{exitday,diag}.py.

## [2026-08-04] research | **ProRealCode library (230 ProOrder strategies) screened as a brick-2 replacement — 0 candidates, and FOUR measurement defects found, each of which had already manufactured a false one.** User: *"scrappe toutes les stratégies gratuites de prorealcode.com/library … indices US/EU, forex, crypto, or … pas de données externes … traduis et backtest en python en suivant à la lettre les règles … je cherche un remplacement de la brique 2, privilégie des stratégies intraday avec beaucoup de trades."* | **ACQUISITION, no scraping**: the site runs an open WordPress REST API — category 7 = `prorealtime-trading-strategies` — so `/wp-json/wp/v2/posts?categories=7` returns all 230 posts with the ProBuilder source inside `<pre>`, in 9 min. (Keep: `wptexturize` rewrites `-` into an en-dash inside some `<pre>` blocks, so the code will not lex until undone.) | **PORT**: a ProBuilder→Python transpiler (`prc_lang.py` + `prc_rt.py`), as with `tv_pine.py`, because hand-porting caps out at a dozen scripts. **The one semantic difference from Pine decides the architecture**: ProBuilder variables PERSIST across bars (an `IF`-assigned variable keeps its previous value; `ONCE` initialises once), so a vectorised compile would silently change the meaning of half the corpus → every variable is a full-length carried-forward array (which also makes `myvar[1]`, legal in ProBuilder, fall out free), with pure expressions hoisted out of the loop (80-95% of lines). Funnel **230 → 129 distinct rules**, filters structural: 25 pyramiding (`CumulateOrders=True`), 16 external data (`CALL` = the ProBuilder iCustom), 14 multi-timeframe, 5 traded volume, 18 non-constant periods, and **5 whose rule is literally not in the file** (variable declared in ProRealTime's optimisation panel, never in the code). | **VALIDATED FIRST, 16 checks**: cross-engine against `mt4_backtest.simulate` (no shared code) = **590/590 and 617/617 trades identical**; synthetic fill/tie-break/cost exactness; **zero same-bar re-entries** (the cadence bug avoided by construction); and the **clock measured, not assumed** — tick volume peaks on the Paris index at **09:00 and 15:30 for GER40, 15:30 for NAS100** (Frankfurt and New York opens), so `time = 090000` in a script lands on the real DAX open. | **THE FINDING — four defects, each large enough to top the table, three in execution and one in COST.** (a) **An imputed stop never PLACED**: 45 of 129 scripts declare no stop (one says `// STOP LOSS : NON`); a 2.5×ATR *denominator* without an attached stop gave fictitious R and unbounded losses (worst −8.07R against a "1R"). (b) **The same-bar gift**: a limit entry fills at a bar's extreme, and granting a limit exit at the *opposite* extreme of the same bar assumes the low came before the high. `The Forex BOUNCE Code` earned **94% of its entire P&L from same-bar round trips** and **every holding period ≥3 bars was negative**; refusing it took GBPUSD M30 **t=+13.72 → +3.13**, EURUSD M15 **+11.88 → −1.98**. Adverse same-bar exits stay allowed (the pessimistic side): the 59 remaining average −1.01R, all stop-outs. (c) **The rollover wick**, measurable with NO rule: on AUDUSD H1 the next bar's open sits **+0.144R above the low of the broker-day's first bar** (R = 0.6% of price) against **−0.024R** on the 22:00 bar. `End of Day forex strategy` places limits across it and read t=+7.9; split by fill hour, **the 23:00-Paris bar carries +182R (t=9.35) and the 00:00 bar +10.5R (t=0.73)** — 100% of the edge on the one bar of the day where the spread is 4–6× wider. Two engine rules followed: a **limit must be penetrated by at least the bar's own quoted spread** (MT5 CFD bars are BID bars, so a buy limit touched by a spread-blown bid-low is a fill the ask never offered), and **the rollover bar never gives a favourable fill**. ⚠️ That guard then had its own bug — on a DAILY series every bar is the first of its broker day, so it silently switched off every D1 take-profit; caught by the session-null diagnostic, cause was `.asi8` being **milliseconds** not nanoseconds on this pandas build. (d) **⚠️ THE COST MODEL WAS WRONG, PROJECT-WIDE, AND WRONG IN THE FLATTERING DIRECTION** — found only because the user asked *"es-tu sûr que tu utilises les bons coûts ?"*. This Pepperstone feed quotes **FX and metals RAW** (EURUSD median bar spread **0 points**, live 2 points = 0.2 pip): a Razor-style account where the broker is paid by a **SEPARATE COMMISSION that appears in no column of the data** — ~€2.6/lot/side on this EUR account ≈ €5.2 round-turn per 100k against ≈€9.2 per pip ≈ **0.6 pip ≈ 6 broker points**. Charging spread + slippage alone priced FX round trips at **0.20–0.40 pip when the all-in cost is 0.8–1.0: two to four times too cheap**, precisely on the instruments where every high-frequency candidate lived. (Indices/crypto/energies are commission-free here — `trade_calc_mode` 2 vs 5 for FX/metals — and were already right.) Second, smaller defect: **one bar's spread was charged for both legs**; each leg now pays its own bar's half-spread. Null, session-null and placebo were all put on the same corrected grid — a benchmark priced cheaper than the strategies it judges flatters every one of them. **Effect: it kills the high-frequency FX candidates at BASE cost, not merely under stress** — RSI-3-QQQ/EURUSD M15 **+2.86 → −0.54**, RSI-FX-KISS/AUDUSD M15 **+2.66 → +1.10**, IBS-QQQ/USDCAD H1 **+3.85 → +1.80**, while the index and the 10-trades/yr candidates barely move. I.e. the +10 pts/side stress test had been doing work the base model should have done. | **RESULTS (all-in cost), 9804 cells (129 rules × 19 assets × D1/H1/M30/M15), 5190 runnable**: t>2 = **78 against 118.1 by theoretical chance (0.66×)** — the MQL4 "fewer winners than chance" result on a fresh corpus — but against the **matched PLACEBO** (identical funnel on random rules matched cell-by-cell on asset, tf, trade count, direction mix, bracket, sequencing, same cost grid), which yields **58.3**, the corpus is **1.34× at t>2 and 2.6× at t>2.9**: it carries something, but little. The matched random-entry null agrees — **median t excess −0.13**, the median rule being *worse* than its own geometry entered at random. **0 cells above t=4, real or placebo.** | **THE TIMEFRAME GRADIENT — the direct answer to the user's brief, and the pass's most useful number: D1 median t −0.11 / 46.7% positive E[R] / 1.56× chance; H1 −1.23 / 21.7% / 0.71×; M30 −1.85 / 17.3% / 0.53×; M15 −2.28 / 15.0% / 0.27×.** Monotone in bar size, and **charging the commission STEEPENED it** (M15 went 0.51× → 0.27×, D1 barely moved) — which exhibits the mechanism rather than arguing it: friction scales with trade count, the gross edge does not. Extends "H1 is below chance, D1 is not" to a second independent corpus. | **BATTERY on 8 shortlisted (t>2 AND beats its own null AND positive R/yr): 0 survive.** Discriminating tests: cost +10 pts/side, fill delayed one bar, clock ±1h, trimmed t. Two got furthest. **`DAX40 H1 Long Trend System` (GER40 H1 / NAS100 M30)** passes the statistics (t=3.12/3.24, p_null 0.000/0.002, cost+10 +2.40, delay1 +3.10, clock-robust, 8/9 yrs) and **fails on the PATH**: standalone maxDD **16.6R for +9.7 R/yr → RoMaD 0.58**, at equal prop risk **−6.0 pts/yr swapping gold, −4.5 adding**, book maxDD 13.8→21.2R; the NAS100 variant is **+0.162 correlated to brick 1** and its trimmed t is −0.56. **The ledger's closed GER40 story reproduced on a different mechanism**, and the corrected cost made it worse (RoMaD 0.83 → 0.58). **`Mean reverting strategy FOREX – AUDCAD` (NZDUSD D1)** passes nearly all of it — t=3.30, p_null 0.000 whole AND recent, cost+10 **+3.15**, delay1 **+3.25**, **8/8 positive years**, |corr| ≤ 0.023 to all four bricks, standalone **RoMaD 1.10 / Sharpe 5.94** (2nd only to IBS 1.59, above gold 0.73), and at equal risk it **improves the book**: swap gold +1.1 pts/yr, **add +2.8** with RoMaD 2.71→2.99 and maxDD *down*, ΔRoMaD P(gain) 93%. **NOT promoted, three reasons, the second decisive:** (1) ~**10 trades/yr on one asset** (OOS median +0.28, 0 of 18 above 2) — the very thinness the user objects to in brick 2, not the intraday replacement asked for; (2) **the author declares NO stop, the 2.5×ATR is MINE, and it is a knife-edge on exactly the admission metric** — t stays positive across the sweep (1.40…3.35) so the edge is not manufactured by the stop, but RoMaD reads 0.28/0.75/0.59/**1.12**/0.80/0.48/0.49 across 1.0…6.0×ATR, so "better path than gold" exists **only at my chosen value**; (3) the author's own 27-point parameter neighbourhood has median t +1.89, 44% above 2, with **its maximum at the published point** (fitted-peak signature; cf. GER40 1.43/11% rejected, Ichimoku 2.20/72% healthy). | **Blind corpus confirmations**: the D1 top is entirely the **Larry Connors reversion family on US indices** (RSI2, Cumulative RSI2, Double 7's, IBS, %Bollinger) = **brick 4's mechanism rediscovered**, and the only crypto survivors are trend = brick 3. `RESEARCH_LOG_PRC.md`, scratchpad/prc_{harvest,lang,rt,compile,verify,run,null,session_null,placebo,battery,shortlist,swap,report}.py.

## [2026-08-06] fix | **Brick 4 (NAS100 IBS) took a live trade risking ~160 € on a 100 € 1R — the SL and the sizing formula are both correct; the cause is the broker's MINIMUM LOT.** User: *"l'algorithme vient de prendre une position sur le NAS … brique 4 ibs, mais il n'y a pas 100€ de risque mais 160€, soit il y a un probleme de taille de position soit un probleme de taille de sl"*. Demo balance is **10 036 €** (`runner.log` startup), so 1R = **100.36 €** (1.00%). NAS100's ideal size for a 2.5×ATR IBS stop is **≈ 0.063 lot**, below the broker's `volume_min` (0.10). `broker._snap_lots` snapped it UP to 0.10 → realised risk ≈ 0.10/0.063 × 100 ≈ **160 € = 1.6R**, and did so **silently** (unlike `risk.lots_for`, which at least warns — but that path is unused; the strategies call `broker.lots_for_risk`). Not a bug in the stop or the formula: NAS100 at 2.5 ATR is simply **not sizeable to 1R on a 10 k€ account** — 0.1 lot (the smallest tradeable) already risks ~1.6R. **FIX (`broker.lots_for_risk` + all 4 bricks):** refactored the per-lot loss into `_loss_per_lot`; the sizer now always **logs a WARNING** with the realised R when the min lot forces risk above 1R, and **SKIPS the entry** (returns 0.0 lots) past `max_risk_R` (config, **1.25R** default) — callers treat `lots<=0` as "skip, mark the day done". Only NAS100 is exposed here (gold 0.01 / crypto 0.03/0.82 are all above their minimums). Verified the branch logic in isolation: 1.60R→SKIP+warn, 1.20R→take 0.10+warn, 1.00R→0.50 no warn, <1R (floor down)→no warn. ⚠️ **The already-open ~160 € NAS position is NOT touched by this** — the fix only guards FUTURE entries. **NOT YET LIVE: the VPS auto-updates from `origin/main`, so this needs a commit + push to take effect.** `edgelab/live/{broker,strategies}.py`, `config_live{,.example}.yaml`, `wiki/system.md`.

## [2026-08-06] ops | **Demo account upsized 10 k€ → 60 k€ to end the min-lot over-risk; 1R is now 600 € at the unchanged 1.00%.** User: *"jai modifie mon compte demo et jai mis 60000 de capital … ajuste le risque par trade a 600"*. No numeric change was needed: `risk_per_trade` is a FRACTION, and with `size_from_account:true` the runner reads the real balance at startup, so `1R = 0.01 × 60000 = 600 €` automatically. **Did NOT set `risk_per_trade: 600`** — that would be 60000% of the account (600 × balance); clarified the config comment in both `config_live.yaml` and `.example` to lock that fraction-vs-euros footgun. At 60 k€ the NAS100 min-lot issue is moot (0.1 lot ≈ 160 € ≈ 0.27R, far under 1R), so the `max_risk_R` cap from the prior fix stays as a net but will not trigger. **Requires a runner restart on the VPS** for the 60 k balance to register (startup log should read `1R = 600.00`). `edgelab/live/config_live{,.example}.yaml`.

## [2026-08-06] fix | **Lot sizing switched from FLOOR to NEAREST-step — Pepperstone NAS100's 0.1-lot grid was biasing every brick-4 trade down ~half a step.** User: *"avec pepperstone demo on peut avoir qu'une decimale sur les lots donc c'est pas precis"*. On the 60 k€ demo (1R = 600 €) NAS100 steps by **0.1 lot ≈ 160 € ≈ 0.27R**, so the ideal ≈ 0.375 lot is unreachable: `_snap_lots`' `floor` took **0.3 lot = 0.80R** (a persistent −20% under-size vs the backtest's 1R), and the just-reprised position sat there. Fix: `_snap_lots` now rounds to the **NEAREST** grid step (`floor(raw/step + 0.5)*step`) → 0.4 lot = 1.07R, and the quantisation error centres on 1R instead of a one-sided downward bias (avg ≈ −0.13R removed). `lots_for_risk` reworked to match: SKIP past `max_risk_R` (1.25R) as before, but now only WARN on a *materially* hot sub-cap size (>1.10R) — routine ±half-step quantisation is silent, so the change doesn't spam the log/Discord. Verified on the real grid (min 0.1 / step 0.1): 0.375→0.4 (silent), exact-1R→0.4, 0.343→0.3 (unbiased), 10 k€ min-lot domination→SKIP, 1.14R→WARN+take. The account-size lever is exhausted (user: demo is already at its 60 k€ max), so nearest-rounding is the only precision gain available — the 0.1-lot grid stays a hard ±13% floor on NAS100 sizing. ⚠️ The already-open 0.3-lot NAS position is unchanged until its normal exit; future entries use nearest. **Needs commit + push; VPS auto-updates from origin/main.** `edgelab/live/broker.py`, `wiki/system.md`.

## [2026-08-05] METHOD CORRECTION (user) + widened screen | **"Pourquoi ne pas tester chaque stratégie qui bat son null même si sur l'actif il y en a moins que la chance ? Il se pourrait très bien qu'une excellente stratégie apparaisse … si le nombre de bonnes strat ne dépasse pas ce qu'on aurait eu par chance alors tu n'y prêtes pas attention."* **The user is right, this ledger already says so (method correction 2026-07-31: the multiple-testing bar is "a FLAG meaning validate this properly, not a filter that bins candidates"), and I had partially violated it.** The count-versus-chance arithmetic is a statement about the ENSEMBLE; one excellent rule buried in 5000 noisy cells moves that count by one and is invisible in it. My ProRealCode pass built a 57-cell / 29-strategy shortlist and then batteried only **8** — the same failure mode as the `tv_battery2` post-mortem (top-20 by raw t, 128 of 148 hits never tested). | **REDONE PROPERLY (`prc_screen.py`), three changes.** (1) **p_null computed on ALL 5190 runnable cells**, not on the t≥2 ones plus a sample. (2) **The gate is p_null, not t** — comparing cells on raw t selects the ones with the friendliest geometry, i.e. those least likely to beat their own null; t has no absolute meaning here since the null median ranges −2.9…+2.1 across assets and brackets. (3) **Every cell clearing the gate gets the stress battery**, not a top-N slice: cheap tests (cost +10 pts, fill delay, clock ±1h, trimmed t, recent-half null) on all of them, the expensive full battery (18-asset OOS, book impact, correlations, bootstrap CI) only on survivors — an order of operations, not a selection. | **RESULT: 222 cells beat their own null at p<0.05 with positive R/yr, across 66 distinct strategies — and 183 of them (82%) had t ≤ 2 and were INVISIBLE to the old gate.** The user's objection was therefore not academic: the widened screen surfaced **5 candidates never previously tested**, 3 of them with t<2. **Which gate does the work, of 222: cost 180, fill delay 172, clock 127, trimmed t 99, recent half 83** — the friction wall quantified as an elimination count. **8 of 222 survive the stress screen; the full battery then kills all 8.** | **The 5 new ones, and how each dies** — `Sell in May, Buy in October`/XAUUSD H1 (+13.9 R/yr, the largest R/yr of the whole pass) is **one trade**: win rate **12%**, best trade **+45.06R** against a worst of −1.05R, **top-3 = 85% of P&L, ex-best-3 t=+0.48, trimmed t=+0.06**. Diagnosis worth keeping: it is a **six-month seasonal position being cut by MY imputed 2.5×ATR(H1) stop after a median of 57 bars** and re-armed; sized to the author's intent (25×ATR → median hold 2522 bars ≈ 6 months) it is **+2.2 R/yr, t=2.45**. `RSI-2`/US2000 D1 RoMaD **0.29**, `RSI 2P`/GBPUSD D1 +0.5 R/yr RoMaD 0.44, `RSI-2`/US500 D1 p_null 0.062 on the precise null — all three **degrade book RoMaD**. `Following the sharks`/GER40 H1 has only 3 years. | **THE ONE GENUINELY NEW LEAD, and the sharpest result of the pass: the Larry Connors RSI-2 FAMILY pools into a real, null-beating sleeve — and still loses at equal risk.** It appeared 4× among the survivors (US2000 ×2, US500, GBPUSD) with consistently positive cross-asset OOS medians (+0.47…+0.78), so it was pooled as ONE sleeve (`prc_pool.py`) and tested against a **matched POOLED null** (same assets, brackets, trade counts, entered at random, pooled identically) — the ledger's "basket illusion" warning tested rather than assumed. **4 legs: n=287, 38 trades/yr, +4.2 R/yr, pooled t=+4.52 against a pooled null of median +0.30 / 95th +1.94 → p=0.0000.** It is not the basket illusion. **But: 35.4% of active days have more than one leg closing and max leg-leg corr +0.664 (the two US2000 variants are the same rule twice), standalone RoMaD 0.74 — IDENTICAL to gold's 0.73 — and at equal prop risk it is −1.5 pts/yr added, −1.6 swapping gold.** | **AND THE REFINEMENT THAT MATTERS MOST — pushing the user's own thesis to its limit falsifies part of it.** Pooling **all 12** Connors cells that beat their null (instead of 4) gives **4122 trades, 483 trades/yr, +10.8 R/yr, pooled t=+3.00 against a null median of −2.85 → p=0.0000** — exactly the high-frequency sleeve the brief asked for, and statistically sound. **Yet standalone maxDD 33.8R → RoMaD 0.32, Sharpe 1.06, and −12.8 pts/yr at equal risk.** The cause is legible in the per-leg table: the two high-frequency legs (`RSI 2P`/EURUSD H1 n=1294 t=0.51, `/BTCUSD M15` n=2154 t=0.17) earn **E[R] = +0.007 and +0.002 R per trade** and beat their null only because **their null is −2.85** — on those cells a random entry loses a lot and the rule loses slightly less. They contribute all of the variance and none of the return. **NEW STANDING RULE: `p_null < 0.05` is NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT — a cell can beat a strongly negative null while having E[R] ≈ 0, so the null gate must be paired with an economic floor (E[R], or R/yr per unit of drawdown). Beating a terrible null is not the same as making money.** Concretely measured: 4 D1 legs → RoMaD 0.74; the same family plus the high-frequency legs → RoMaD 0.32. **Adding frequency by admitting everything that beats its null actively destroys the sleeve** — which is also the cleanest available answer to "privilégie des stratégies intraday avec beaucoup de trades". Still 0 new brick, but the shortlist is now exhaustive rather than a top-8. `RESEARCH_LOG_PRC.md`, scratchpad/prc_screen.py, prc_pool.py.

## [2026-08-07] research | **TJR London Fakeout (Pine v5) ported to Python + backtested on Pepperstone (MT5-live) as a brick-2 replacement — 0 edge, gross AND net.** User sent the "TJR London Fakeout Strategy FREE" TradingView strategy, asked to convert it to Python, backtest on Pepperstone data connected to MT5, and judge it against brick 2. **Mechanism** (a liquidity-sweep REVERSAL, distinct from the plain London *breakout* already killed 2026-07-31): mark the Asia range (00:00–08:00 London); during London (08:00–12:00) wait for a wick to SWEEP the Asia high (→ short bias) or low (→ long bias); then require an opposite **break of structure** (close beyond the last confirmed `ta.pivothigh/low(2,2)` swing); enter at that BOS bar's close; **SL = the sweep extreme, TP = the opposite Asia side**, 1R = |entry−SL|; one trade per London session; bracket held to SL/TP (`closeAtLondonEnd` default false). Ported to the letter (`scratchpad/tjr_bt.py`): confirmed causal pivots (known swingLen bars late), the exact sweep/BOS state machine, entry-at-close (`process_orders_on_close`), intrabar exits resolved on the signal bars with a **pessimistic** stop-wins-ties tie-break. Fresh Pepperstone bars pulled live from the PepperstoneUK-Demo terminal (`scratchpad/tjr_pull.py`): 15 London-relevant assets (9 FX majors, XAUUSD, GER40/FRA40/UK100/NAS100/US500), **M15 2022–2026** (~4 yr) and **M5 2025–2026** (the 100k-bar terminal cap sets the depth). Net of realistic cost incl. the raw-account FX/metals **commission** (6 pts RT). | **RESULT — no edge, and it is NOT a cost story.** NET M15 pooled: FX t=−3.49, indices t=−3.65, **ALL 15 t=−4.54 (−3.1 R/yr per asset-year, PF 0.84)**; only **3 of 15** assets positive and **none** at t>2. **GROSS of all cost the ceiling is still ~0**: FX t=−0.98, indices t=−1.06, ALL t=−0.98 — the fade+BOS is a coin flip (win 54–60% but E[R]<0: the sweep-extreme stop is wider than the Asia-side TP, so frequent small wins < rare big losses). M5 corroborates (finer TF, more friction): FX net t=−4.59, ALL t=−3.27. **The one non-negative cell is XAUUSD** (M15 net +4.9 R/yr but **t=1.02**, gross +6.6 t=1.37; minRR≥2 → t=1.55, n=61) — **not significant, RoMaD 0.29 ≪ brick 2's 0.73**, and it is the documented **gold-drift/geometry** artifact (a long-biased fade collecting the 2022–26 bull; a random long-only gold bracket already scores t≈+2 per the geometry-baseline note), not a real edge. **Verdict: not a brick-2 candidate — fails the t>2 gate on every asset, has no gross edge to begin with, and its only positive is gold drift with a worse path than the sleeve it would replace.** Reconfirms the FX/index intraday-reversion wall (bid-ask-bounce-sized edge < friction) on a THIRD sweep-style setup, and extends the killed "London breakout" row to its fakeout variant: adding the BOS confirmation does not create an edge. `scratchpad/tjr_bt.py`, `scratchpad/tjr_pull.py`, ledger row.

VWAP built on MT5 **tick_volume** (no real volume — flagged). **3) Day Trading Rauf** (London 01:12-02:12 + NY 08:12-09:12 range sweep → 3-candle-reversal confirmation → fade, SL=sweep extreme+4tick, TP=opposite range side, forced-close 16:00) — NET ALL-15 **t=-6.84**; **GROSS t=-2.10** (negative even free). Nth confirmation; brick 2 stands. `scratchpad/qc_bt.py`.

## [2026-08-07] research | **Perry Kaufman, *Trading Systems and Methods* (5th ed.) mined for an intraday edge — the noise law measured and found ~8x too small, Kaufman's own intraday system falsified GROSS, and the one net survivor is brick 1 again.** User: analyse the book in the repo (`TSaM.pdf`), design + validate an exploitable day-trading edge on forex / XAUUSD / US+EU indices / crypto, OHLCV only, mandatory SL at entry, everything flat before the session close, no martingale/grid. | **Extraction.** `pdftotext -layout` → 3.4 MB / 58k lines, TOC recovered. Four passages carry mechanical content: **ch.1 pp.10-14 + ch.20 pp.867-871** (the efficiency ratio as a measure of NOISE, explicitly not volatility, and Fig. 1.7's law: low noise → trend pays, high noise → reversion pays; equity indices are the noisiest sector); **ch.16 pp.753-775** (TSM Midday Support & Resistance — the book's ONLY fully-specified intraday strategy, stated for 15/30-min bars — plus ORB and Crabel's inside-day precondition); **ch.17 pp.779-798** (KAMA, `sc=[ER*(fast−slow)+slow]^2`; Meyers' Adaptive Intraday Breakout with the published QQQ params n=6/a=0.75/0.50/1.05); **ch.23 pp.1050-1072** (stops must be volatility-scaled, never fixed). | **Engine.** New `scratchpad/kauf_lib.py`, identical execution model to the rest of the project plus one thing none of the existing engines had: a **per-trade SESSION DEADLINE**, because "purely intraday" is not the same as "capped at N bars" (a trade opened 3 bars before the flat time must die there, one opened at the open may live all day). **Verified 60/60 before any result was read**: with the deadline OFF it reproduces `gold_lib.outcomes` **bar-for-bar, max diff 0.0e+00**, exit indices identical, across 5 brackets x 2 directions x 3 symbols; with it ON an independently-written explicit per-trade loop reproduces it on 4,000 random entry bars per cell (0 exit mismatches, 0 missing). Data: Pepperstone via the live MT5 bridge — M15 resampled from the M1 cache for the 11 symbols reaching back to 2018/2019, fresh M15/M5 pulled for the rest (`kauf_pull.py`), server clock → true UTC before any session logic. **Fill fidelity is exact here**: the headline bracket has NO target, so the only intrabar barrier is the stop and no bar can straddle two — the exp-005 "bar backtests over-count" warning does not bite. | **(1) The noise law, measured rather than argued.** Design built to be immune to the geometry baseline: ONE trigger (n-bar intraday range breakout at the close), traded in the SAME direction in both states, split only by the noise state — a difference of means cancels drift exactly, so no null is required. The state is read at bar **t−n**, before the broken range, because reading ER on the breakout bar is circular (a break IS a directional move and mechanically inflates ER over its own window; the first pass showed the symptom: 3,570 "low-noise" events against 331 "high-noise"). **405 cells (17 symbols x 3 timeframe/source combinations x 3 lookbacks x 3 brackets): median law_t = +0.26, median OLS slope_t = +0.30, 57.3% of cells positive, asset-level medians 28/45 positive → binomial p = 0.135. Median effect = +0.0103 R/trade. Median friction on the same grid = 0.079 R/trade.** The sign Kaufman predicts is there; it is **~8x too small to pay the toll**. By class: us_idx +0.46, eu_idx +0.40, crypto +0.31, forex +0.17, gold −0.44. His evidence is daily bars ACROSS markets; the law does not survive the move to intraday bars WITHIN a market. | **(2) Kaufman's own intraday strategy is falsified — and NOT on cost.** Midday S/R ported to the letter (morning high/low to 12:00 local; afternoon: buy the lower 25% of that range, sell the upper 25%, go with a close beyond it), with the two departures the brief forces and the book omits (mandatory ATR stop — his is stop-and-reverse with none — and the forced session flat). **1,275 cells: median t = −3.29, ZERO cells at t>2, 4.7% with positive E[R]**; per leg both −3.84 / fade −3.57 / break −2.52; best cell anywhere t=+1.02. **The gross run settles it**: cost set to zero, median gross t = both −0.26, **fade −0.55**, break +0.02, and only 5-6 of 425 gross cells reach t>2. On the US indices Kaufman explicitly names as its natural home ("more noise … contributing to its ability to reverse at a support or resistance level") the gross median is **+0.05**. There is no edge to lose. His +$268/trade over 8,404 S&P trades is stated **"1 contract each, no costs"** — that caveat is doing more work than the strategy. | **(3) The friction floor, quantified.** Across 2,975 (rule x bracket x symbol) cells the median cost is **0.079 R/trade on M15** and median t moves **−2.7 net → −0.02 gross**: the whole distribution is a friction shift, not a dead edge plus noise. Clean specimen — **Meyers' Adaptive Intraday Breakout on USDJPY** at the published params, SL 0.75xATR, n=8,527: **gross t = +5.07 → net t = −0.55**. A real gross signal, smaller than the spread; widening to SL 2.0xATR gives gross +4.69 → net +2.01, i.e. the surviving "edge" is a stop-width choice. **The one asymmetry in the entire study: gross median t is positive for EVERY breakout rule only on us_idx** (kaer_follow +1.70, kaer_switch +1.87, kama_flip +1.39, meyers +0.96) and NEGATIVE on forex (−1.20, −0.89) — FX has no signal to lose, gross or net. | **(4) The one net survivor, and why it is not a 5th brick.** Sweep of **3,825 cells** (17 symbols x 9 rules x 25 brackets): median t −2.66, **46 cells at t>2 — BELOW the ~88 chance gives at this cell count** — but **34 of the 46 are US indices and 30 are NAS100 alone**. Headline: NAS100 M15, follow a 10-bar intraday range breakout when the efficiency ratio at the signal bar sits in the top tercile of its trailing 20-session history, **stop 2.0xATR14, no target, flat 15:55 NY** → **n=2505 (293/yr), +28.7 R/yr, PF 1.189, t=+3.19, maxDD 25.4R, RoMaD 1.13, 8/9 positive years**, 1R median 52.7x the spread (5th pct 16x), split-half 2.24 / 2.28 (stable, right way round). **Placebo divisor 24x** — the identical 25-bracket grid on matched random signals gives **0/25 cells at t>2 across 3 replicates against 12/25 real**, by a wide margin the highest ratio this project has measured (prior corpus passes ran at **1.2x**). ⚠️ **Honest relabelling**: reading ER at t−n (the LAW) gives t=1.93; reading it at the signal bar — which is Kaufman's own convention, since KAMA's `sc_t` uses `ER_t` — gives t=3.19. What pays is therefore a **move-QUALITY filter (buy an efficient one-way break rather than a whipsaw)**, not a regime filter. **REJECTED on three counts.** (a) **Cross-asset replication fails**, same rule and bracket, nothing refitted: NAS100 +3.19 / US500 +1.64 / GER40 +1.30 / US30 +0.00 / FRA40 −0.17 / US2000 −1.56 / UK100 −1.72 / XAU −0.20 / EURUSD −3.26 / BTC +0.27 / ETH −0.26 → sign-robust on the big US/DE indices, significant on exactly one; the same verdict the GER40 ORB got. (b) **Not decorrelated — it is brick 1's family**: corr **+0.370** to brick 1 and **40% of its trading days also close a brick-1 trade**, against ≤0.03 for every brick-brick pair in the live book (gold +0.006, crypto −0.019). This is now the **fourth independent re-derivation of brick 1's mechanism** (after Big Daddy Max ORB and the two sos_bt passes), from a completely different source. (c) **Adding it barely pays**: at equal risk (−10% static floor, 2x historical maxDD) book as-is **10.3%/yr**, +KAER@0.5R **11.7%** (+1.4 pts, RoMaD 2.06→2.35), +KAER@1R **10.1%** (worse — the correlated drawdown eats the return), swapping brick 1 for it 10.2% (flat); and it is cost-fragile past +10 broker points/side (t 3.19 → 2.40 at +5 → 1.61 at +10). Its standalone RoMaD **1.22 does beat brick 1's 0.79**, so the live question this raises is "**is this a better brick 1?**", not "is this a 5th brick". | **METHOD KEEPER — a second null.** The random-entry null does not control WHEN you trade: a breakout bar has a higher ATR relative to the spread, so the same bracket costs less in R, and a cell can beat that null purely by trading at cheaper moments (measured: the random-entry null sits at −0.5…−2.0 on this grid for exactly that reason). Added **NULL B = sign permutation on the cell's own signal bars** at the same long fraction — timing, volatility and friction held fixed, only direction randomised. Null B is often strongly POSITIVE where Null A is negative (NAS100 **+1.24**, the drift a long-biased breakout set collects) and is a much harder bar; the headline cell clears it at p=0.005-0.010. **Standing rule added to the ledger: beating the random-entry null alone is not evidence of a DIRECTIONAL edge — report both.** | **0 new brick; 1 forward-test candidate filed as a brick-1 variant.** Deliverable for the user: full spec + `scratchpad/kaufman_intraday_backtest.py`, a standalone pandas/numpy CLI backtest (indicators, session flat, ATR bracket, R metrics, Sharpe/maxDD/PF/win-rate, per-year table, matched null). `RESEARCH_LOG_KAUFMAN.md`, ledger row + 🎯 method note, scratchpad/kauf_*.py.

## [2026-08-08] analysis | **Best combination of the six available sleeves — and it is NOT "add everything": shrinking the DECAYING brick 3 to 0.5R and adding Keltner at 0.5R strictly dominates the current book, while KAER drops out as an addition.** User: *"pars du principe que je n'ai rien forward-testé encore, quelle est la meilleure combinaison de briques (briques actuelles + KAER + Keltner)"*. Six sleeves on the canonical calendar index (`build_daily_R` for b1-b4 at live cadence, `run_kaer` for KAER, `tsam_rules` Keltner at its **floored** 25x-spread config), weights swept over {0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0} = **15,625 books** (`scratchpad/book_optimise.py`). | **⚠️ THE HEADLINE METRIC BREAKS DOWN HERE, and the MC exposes it.** Ranked on the project's usual *%/yr at equal risk* = (0.10 / 2·maxDD)·R/yr, the top 15 books are all "**b4 @1R + everything else @0.25**" at 18-20%/yr — because that metric assumes you lever up to fill the drawdown cushion, and a tiny-maxDD book levers ~15x, which breaks system.md's own **"never above 1% per trade"** rule. The Monte-Carlo shows what those books really are: the in-sample optimum has **P(PASS) = 50.5% at 0.50%/trade** (too slow to reach +15%) and withdraws 7.4%/yr. **Standing correction: rank books at a FIXED risk per trade on the E[withdrawn] vs P(ruin) frontier, not on unconstrained %/yr.** | **THE OUT-OF-SAMPLE WEIGHT TEST.** Weights chosen on the first half, scored on the second: the in-sample optimum lands at the **99th percentile of 500 random weightings** (median 9.7%, 90th 12.9%) — so weight optimisation does transfer, it is not pure curve-fitting. But the level tells the real story: **the current book gets BETTER out of sample (11.5% → 13.4%) while every six-sleeve book gets WORSE (19.0% → 10.7-13.8%)** — the signature of the two new sleeves' contribution being concentrated in the early half. | **PER-SLEEVE DECAY (R/yr, 1st half → 2nd half, split 2022-07-14): b1 +8.0 → +17.6 (x2.19), b2 +1.9 → +3.1 (x1.68), b4 +5.1 → +4.5 (x0.88), KELT +20.4 → +14.4 (x0.71), KAER +35.4 → +26.4 (x0.74), and b3 +17.8 → +5.7 (x0.32).** Brick 3 is by far the fastest-decaying sleeve in the book — which system.md already flags independently ("brick 3 is the weak link, ex-2020 t=1.30, negative in 2024 and 2025"), so this is a confirmation, not a fresh fit. | **THE ANSWER — config E: b1@1R, b2@1R, `b3@0.5R`, b4@1R, `KELT@0.5R`, NO KAER.** It **strictly dominates the current book on every axis**, full sample and recent half alike: R/yr +32.0 → **+34.8**, maxDD 14.3 → **13.0**, Sharpe 1.95 → **2.21**, RoMaD 2.24 → 2.69; and on the 2nd half at 0.50%/trade, **P(PASS) 94.1% → 97.2%, E[withdrawn] 16.5% → 18.4%, P(ruin) 1.8% → 1.3%**. Mechanism: swap half of the decaying crypto-DAILY sleeve for half of a decorrelated crypto-HOURLY one (monthly corr KELT-b3 **+0.073**, KELT-b4 **−0.179**). | **The income ladder if more return is wanted, at 0.50%/trade on the 2nd half** (income / ruin): **E 18.4%/1.3%** → **B (A+KELT@0.5) 20.0%/3.0%** → H (b3@0.5+KAER@0.5+KELT@0.5) 24.8%/7.6% → **D (A+KAER@0.5+KELT@0.5) 26.2%/10.3%**. Everything past B buys income at roughly 1:1 against ruin. | **KAER DROPS OUT AS AN ADDITION and the reason is coherent with its own deployment rationale**: as an *addition* it costs +5.8 pts of ruin for +6.4 pts of income (config C) because of its **+0.353 monthly correlation to brick 1** and its 25.3 R standalone maxDD; as a **replacement for brick 1** (measured 2026-08-07) it was the opposite — +1.4 pts/yr at equal risk with ruin FALLING 1.7% → 1.0%. KAER's value proposition is *being a better brick 1*, not being a fifth sleeve, which is exactly how it was deployed (0.5R, in parallel, to settle that question). | **Caveats: (1)** config E is selected partly on the second half, the same data that shows b3's decay — mitigated but not removed by system.md's independent flag; **(2)** Keltner is not forward-tested and is 1 cell of 722 crypto-H1 survivors; **(3)** no change to the live book is recommended on this analysis alone — it is a sizing hypothesis for the forward test, not an instruction. `scratchpad/book_optimise.py`, `RESEARCH_LOG_KAUFMAN2.md`.

## [2026-08-08] analysis | **TWO BOOKS, ONE ACCOUNT: an aggressive book for the CHALLENGE and a defensive one once FUNDED is measurably better than any single-book plan — the user's idea, confirmed.** User: *"est-ce que ça pourrait être pertinent d'utiliser 2 books différents en challenge et une fois funded — D (A + KAER@0.5 + KELT@0.5) à 1%/trade en challenge, E (b3→0.5R + KELT@0.5) à 0.5%/trade en funded"*. It generalises what system.md already recommends on SIZE ("pass at 1.0% for speed, drop to 0.50% once funded") to the SLEEVE MIX, and the reason it works is that the two phases have genuinely different objective functions: **the challenge is a FIRST-PASSAGE problem** (touch +15% before −10%; a failure costs a FEE, not the account, so speed dominates return-per-drawdown), **funded is a RUIN problem** (the account is an asset with an expected lifetime; E[withdrawn/yr] against P(losing it)). A book that is wrong for one can be right for the other. | **METHOD** (`scratchpad/two_phase.py`): one bootstrapped path per replicate runs the challenge on the challenge book at its risk until pass or breach, retries immediately on failure (fee + clock keeps running, so a fragile challenge book is penalised twice — in fees AND in funded days lost), then runs the remainder of a 2-year horizon funded on the funded book with monthly payouts and the −10% static floor. 20k replicates, 14-day blocks, same conventions as `monte_carlo_static`. | **RESULT (2-year horizon; full sample / recent half): the proposal wins.** D@1.00% → E@0.50%: **retiré 30.2% / 30.4%**, P(ruine) **1.6% / 2.9%**, délai médian **2.6 / 2.8 mois**, **20.6 / 20.3 mois funded**, 1.13 / 1.18 essais. Against the best single-book plans: E@1.00%→E@0.50% **27.2 / 27.9%** at 1.4 / 2.6% ruin (4.4 / 4.3 mo to pass); A@1.00%→A@0.50% (the current book) **24.5 / 24.0%** at 3.2 / 3.3% ruin (4.8 / 4.8 mo). **So +3.0 pts of two-year income over the best single-book plan for +0.2 pts of ruin, and +6 pts over the current book with ruin HALVED.** | **THE MECHANISM IS EXACTLY THE ONE INTENDED, and it is legible in one column: the challenge is cleared in 2.6 months instead of 4.4-4.8, which buys ~2 extra months of funded time (20.6 vs 18.6 vs 18.0) — and switching to E for the funded phase keeps ruin at 1.6% instead of D's own 12.3% (19.3% on the recent half).** Staying in D after funding is the trap: same +46.8% withdrawn but **12.3% ruin**, i.e. the aggressive book earns its keep only while a failure is refundable. | **The −5% daily rule never binds**: worst days are A −3.07 R, D −3.58 R, E −2.52 R, F −3.39 R, so at 1%/trade the worst day is −3.6% at most. That is what makes 1.00% affordable in the challenge at all. | **Variants worth knowing**: **D@0.75% → E@0.50%** gives 28.5 / 28.6% at 1.5 / 2.6% ruin with **1.06 / 1.10 attempts** — a third fewer retries, so it is the better choice if the challenge fee is material relative to the account; F@1.00%→E@0.50% ≈ the proposal (29.7 / 30.1%); the conservative E@0.75%→E@0.50% is the cheapest in fees (1.02 attempts) and the slowest (6.1 mo), at 24.5 / 25.3%. | **Cost of the plan: +0.08 to +0.11 expected challenge attempts** vs a single-book plan — the fee is the price of the speed, and it should be priced against ~2 extra funded months. **Caveats: (1)** it assumes KAER and KELT perform as backtested and NEITHER is forward-tested — if both are duds, D@1% degenerates towards A@1% and the plan is not harmful, only slower; **(2)** P(funded) is ~100% for every plan over 2 years because retries are allowed, so the differentiator is attempts + speed, not pass probability — do not read the P(funded) column as a quality signal; **(3)** operationally it is a config change at funding time (disable KAER, halve brick 3), not a code change. `scratchpad/two_phase.py`.

## [2026-08-08] reference | **P(valider le challenge) avec le livre D, table complete.** D = b1+b2+b3+b4 @1R + KAER@0.5R + KELT@0.5R (+56.1 R/an, maxDD 18.6 R, RoMaD 3.01, pire jour -3.58 R). C'est ainsi qu'une sleeve porte un week-end qu'elle n'a jamais accepte: **13% des barres de sortie de la brique 2 et 20% de celles de la brique 4 sont des vendredis** (56 des 274 sorties non-stop de b4 remplissent a une ouverture du lundi). La sortie or du 05/08 a 01:01 en etait l'illustration. | **⚠️ PAS 5 MINUTES MAIS 10, et la donnee le dicte: du lundi au jeudi ces symboles cotent jusqu'a 23:55 serveur, mais LE VENDREDI la derniere barre M5 est 23:50 — la seance finit A 23:55 serveur (22:55 Paris) pile.** Un decalage de 5 min tirerait donc exactement sur la cloture du vendredi, le seul jour ou le week-end est en jeu. **23:50 serveur (22:50 Paris) est la derniere fenetre vivante tous les jours de la semaine.** Reglable via `rollover_lead_min` (`config_live.example.yaml`). | **COUT MESURE — BRIQUE 2: neutre en moyenne, nettement plus serre en dispersion.** Erreur de suivi contre la sortie `c[xi]` du backtest, sur 89 sorties couvertes par le flux M10, 8.5 ans: **22:50 = -0.43 R au total (moyenne -0.0048, sd 0.045, |max| 0.14)** contre **remplissage actuel a la reouverture = -0.40 R (moyenne -0.0045, sd 0.066, |max| 0.50)**. Sur les 11 cas week-end la reouverture du lundi sort a +0.46 R contre -0.35 R, mais avec sd 0.168 contre 0.052 — soit t≈0.83 sur 11 observations, du bruit, pas un edge. **On echange donc une moyenne identique contre un pire cas divise par 3.5.** | **BRIQUE 4: le plafond de 30 barres N'A JAMAIS TIRE — 0 sortie sur 287** (262 `ibs`, 25 `stop`), l'IBS se resout toujours avant. (la politique de timing appartient au driver, pas au signal); b2 et b4 restructurees comme b3 la veille — **sortie evaluee a chaque passe, entree toujours une fois par jour serveur**, avec la garde `_acted_day` en `<=` pour qu'une cloture anticipee ne puisse pas etre suivie d'une reentree anticipee. | **VERIFICATION**: `verify_time_exits()` passe de 3 a 5 controles — (d) sur les **126** barres de sortie reelles de la brique 2 l'anticipation tire 126/126 la ou la regle du rollover tire 0/126 (elle tire le lendemain), dont **16 vendredis**; (e) sur 32 entrees synthetiques du vrai calendrier NAS100 la sortie au rollover tombe a entree+31 barres et l'anticipation exactement une barre plus tot. ## [2026-08-09] analysis | **COMMISSIONS FTMO CHIFFREES: elles coutent -22% de l'edge de KELT et ~-4% du livre, et TOUT tient dans une formule — cout en R = cotes x taux / stop_pct.** Utilisateur: *"je n'arrive pas a trouver les swaps, par contre les commissions ftmo pour xauusd sont 0.0007% gbp par lot, pour les indices il est ecrit 0% de commission par lot, et pour crypto 0.0325% gbp par lot (j'avais fait un compte en gbp)"*. **Ce sont bien des COMMISSIONS et elles sont PAR COTE** — confirme par FTMO (`ftmo.com/en/trading-crypto-and-indices-examples-explained/`, `ftmo.com/en/blog/zero-commissions-on-indices/`): crypto **0.0325%/cote** du notionnel converti en devise du compte, metaux **0.0007%/cote** (0.0014% aller-retour), indices **0%**. Les swaps sont une TROISIEME ligne, distincte, toujours introuvable. | **LE MECANISME EST LE POINT IMPORTANT, PAS LE CHIFFRE: une commission est proportionnelle au NOTIONNEL alors que le livre raisonne en R, et notionnel/risque = 1/stop_pct.** Donc `cout_R = 2 x taux / stop_pct`: **le meme taux fait mal en proportion INVERSE de la serre du stop**. C'est exactement la meme quantite que celle qui pilote la marge (cf. l'entree marge du meme jour) — un stop serre achete du notionnel, et le notionnel se paie deux fois: en marge et en commission. | **RESULTAT — une seule sleeve paie: KELT.** stop median 2.95% -> **0.0220 R/trade x 153 trades/an = -3.75 R/an, soit 17.35 -> 13.60 R/an (-22%), t 2.99 -> 2.35, PF 1.22 -> 1.16**. KELT passe sous +10 R/an des 3.5 bps/nuit.** La brique 3 encaisse les memes taux sans broncher (BTC 6.41 -> 5.15 a 3 bps, 2.51 a 10 bps; ETH 5.50 -> 4.55 / 2.60) parce que son stop est 3x plus large. **Donc la question "KELT vaut-elle son deploiement" ne se tranche pas au backtest mais au releve de swap FTMO — c'est UN chiffre a aller chercher.** | **OU TROUVER LE SWAP (la commission n'est pas le swap): MT5 -> Ctrl+M -> clic droit sur le symbole -> Specification -> champs `Swap long` / `Swap short` / `Swap type` (Points, Percentage, Money, Interest yearly) / `3-day swap` (le jour ou il est triple).** Si `Swap type` vaut Percentage ou Interest, la valeur est ANNUELLE -> diviser par 360 ou 365. **Methode definitive et gratuite: ouvrir 0.01 lot BTCUSD sur l'essai/demo FTMO, laisser passer 00:00 serveur, lire la colonne `Swap` de l'onglet Trade** — et recommencer sur la nuit du mercredi pour le triple. **Hypothese a verifier: un swap crypto affiche a 0 chez FTMO serait coherent avec un financement deja incorpore dans les 0.0325%/cote** (6.5 bps aller-retour, c'est gros) — si c'est le cas KELT reste a 13.60 R/an et le probleme disparait. | **A FAIRE: injecter ces taux dans `CostModel` (spread + slippage + commission aujourd'hui, aucune commission proportionnelle par symbole cote FTMO, aucun swap).** `scratchpad/ftmo_commissions.py`.

Donc **peu de trades et un 1R grand devant le spread**, pas un filtre de plus. (2) *Le seul edge BRUT consistant est la continuation de cassure intraday sur indices US* (kaer_follow +1.70, kaer_switch +1.87, kama_flip +1.39; forex negatif BRUT). (3) *Le Midday S/R du livre n'a aucun edge brut* (-0.26) donc on ne le reprend pas. (4) *KAER est rejetee pour 293 trades/an, une replication cross-actifs qui echoue et corr +0.370 a la brique 1* — on vise donc ~60 trades/an sur un ancrage DIFFERENT. **COMPOSITION (5 chapitres):** niveau ancre sur la seance = opening range de la 1re heure (ch.16, un niveau REALISE et non une projection ATR autour de l'ouverture, qui est la brique 1); precondition d'ENROULEMENT de Crabel (ch.16) = on ne prend la cassure que si l'OR est etroit par rapport a ses 60 dernieres seances; qualite du mouvement par l'efficiency ratio **lu a la barre de signal** (ch.1/17/20 — la distinction etablie par la passe precedente: lu en t-n il teste la *loi* et vaut t=1.93, lu au signal il mesure la *qualite de la cassure achetee* et vaut t=3.19); stop adaptatif SL = k x ATR14 (ch.23); plancher 1R >= 25 spreads (lecon KELT). `scratchpad/inx_tsm.py`. | **RESULTAT — LA GRILLE ENTIERE, PAS SON MAXIMUM: 792 cellules (11 actifs x 9 variantes de signal x 8 brackets). t>2: 14 observees contre ~18 attendues par hasard. t>2.5: 1 contre ~4.9. t>2.9: ZERO. t max 2.51, alors que la barre du maximum de 792 tests est ~3.3-3.5.** La grille produit MOINS de survivants que le hasard — la signature exacte que le ledger enregistre deja pour le FX et les indices. ## [2026-08-10] refactor | **BUG LIVE CORRIGE — la brique 4 ne parvenait pas a SORTIR de sa position pendant ~3 jours, et le runner ecrivait "order placed" a chaque echec.** Signale par l'utilisateur: *"l'algorithme me dit ca sur le vps mais rien ne s'est passe sur mt5"* a propos de `NasIbsStrategy: market reopened, order placed (waited 0 days 00:10:22)`. | **DIAGNOSTIC: ce n'etait pas une entree manquee mais une SORTIE bloquee.** Avec une position ouverte, le seul point de `step()` capable de lever `MarketClosed` est `broker.close()`. Le log du VPS montre six tentatives le 2026-08-09 (02:21, 13:52, 15:22, 16:52, 17:52, 22:50), chacune suivie **21 secondes plus tard** — exactement un cycle de poll — d'un "market reopened, order placed". Plus un echec dur le 2026-08-08 14:08 (`close order_send failed`). Le ticket 82489480 (NAS100 LONG 0.40, entre le 2026-08-06) est reste ouvert du 7 au 9. | **CAUSE RACINE, une seule ligne: `self._acted_day = bday` etait pose AVANT `broker.close()`.** La premiere passe du jour boursier consommait donc l'unique tentative de sortie de la journee; toutes les passes suivantes ressortaient au garde `first_pass_today` sans retenter. Sur un week-end cela fait trois jours de portage non voulu. Le docstring de `_EntryRetryGuard` promettait pourtant *"Only ENTRIES are bounded. An exit is never abandoned"* — l'intention etait juste, l'implementation la contredisait. | **CAUSE AGGRAVANTE: le message du runner etait un faux positif par construction.** Il etait emis des que `step()` revenait sans lever `MarketClosed`, ce qui ne prouve rien: `step()` compte ~6 sorties silencieuses legitimes (pas de signal, jour deja traite, barre pas encore imprimee, garde de risque, lot nul). Le bug avait 3 jours et etait invisible pour cette raison exacte. | **CORRECTIF (3 fichiers):** (1) **deux marqueurs de jour au lieu d'un** — `_acted_day` (= ne pas OUVRIR sur cette barre) reste pose des qu'une position est vue, ce qui preserve la garantie `cadence='live'` contre un stop rempli en cours de passe; le nouveau `_managed_day` (= la decision de sortie a ABOUTI) n'est pose qu'apres le retour de la tentative, donc un refus laisse la journee non resolue et la passe suivante retente. Applique aux trois briques a rollover (b2, b3, b4); b1/KAER/KELT n'avaient pas le defaut, leur branche de sortie tourne deja a chaque passe. (2) **`Broker.orders_sent`**, compteur d'ordres reellement executes, diffe par le runner autour de `step()`: le message dit desormais `NO order sent on this pass` quand rien n'est parti. (3) **journalisation d'echec limitee** (`runner._log_failure`): trace complete une fois, puis une ligne toutes les 15 min tant que le MEME echec se repete — necessaire *parce que* les sorties retentent maintenant, sinon un refus persistant sur un marche OUVERT ecrirait une trace toutes les 20 s et ferait tourner l'historique utile de `runner.log`. | **VERIFICATION: deux tests de regression ecrits qui rejouent le scenario reel** (position ouverte, signal de sortie IBS actif, broker refusant N fois). Resultats sur les 3 briques: refus 5x -> **6 tentatives de close** (une par passe) puis sortie effective, contre **1 seule** avant le correctif; refus infini -> 200 tentatives sur 200 passes, jamais abandonnee; marche ouvert -> exactement 1 close, pas de martelage; et le runner n'annonce plus d'ordre qu'il n'a pas envoye. `python -m edgelab.live.verify` repasse integralement (brick1..4, KAER, KELT, time-exits = PASS) — attendu, il ne touche ni au cadencement ni a un broker qui refuse. | **LECON A RETENIR, elle depasse ce repo: une ligne de log doit affirmer ce qui a ete OBSERVE, pas ce que le chemin de code laisse supposer.** `verify` ne pouvait pas attraper ce bug: il controle la fidelite signal/cadence sur barres en cache et n'exerce jamais un broker qui refuse. | Fichiers: `edgelab/live/strategies.py`, `edgelab/live/runner.py`, `edgelab/live/broker.py`; [[system]] section "Incident 2026-08-07->09".

## [2026-08-10] analysis | **⭐ BATTERIE COMPLETE SUR LES 352 SIGNAUX AYANT TOUCHE t>2 (106 960 cellules, placebo apparie): LE RAPPORT BRUT DE 11x EST UN ARTEFACT DE SELECTION; a preselection egale il tombe a 1.35x, et TOUT l'effet est NAS100 (3.57x) tandis que les CINQ AUTRES ACTIFS sont a 0.69x, c'est-a-dire SOUS le hasard.** Demande utilisateur: *"backtest toutes les strategies au-dessus de t>2 peu importe s'il y en a moins que par chance ... avec tous les tests qu'on fait passer on devrait etre en mesure de discerner la chance d'un edge"*, avec le rappel *"uniquement des strategies intraday"*. | **COHORTE ENFIN COMPLETE: 352 signaux = 258 Pine + 92 regles TSaM/seance + 2 MQL.** `battery_all.py` (ecrit la veille, jamais lance) n'en couvrait que 260: son `cohort()` ne globait que `corpus_*.parquet` et `mql_*.parquet`, et **ratait les 52 050 cellules du sweep TSaM/regles de seance qui vivent dans `part_<SYM>_<TF>.parquet`** — 92 signaux a t>2 manquaient. | **DISPOSITIF: 9 brackets PRE-DECLARES (SL 1.0/1.5/2.0 x TP none/1R/2R) au lieu d'un seul** — re-tester tout le monde a SL1.5 sans cible tuait mecaniquement tout signal dont le t>2 venait d'une cellule avec cible, ce qui est un faux negatif et non une information. Prendre le meilleur des 9 est une selection, mais **le placebo prend aussi le meilleur de ses 9**, donc le rapport reste calibre: le placebo ne teste pas un signal, il teste la PROCEDURE. Batterie par (signal, actif, bracket): n>=40, t>2, les DEUX demi-echantillons positifs, t>1 a +10 pts/cote, Null A 300 tirages, Null B 300 permutations, puis replication cross-actifs. Intraday strict verifie sur donnees: **18 369 trades tires, 0 sortie hors de la seance d'entree**, detention max 24 barres pour 25 barres de seance. | ⚠️ **TROIS DEFAUTS DE METHODE TROUVES ET CORRIGES EN COURS DE ROUTE, chacun changeait le resultat.** (1) **Le placebo n'appariait pas l'HEURE DE SEANCE**: les survivants concentrent 9-14 % de leurs entrees sur la barre d'ouverture contre 4.8 % pour un tirage uniforme, et Null A avait le meme angle mort -> `battery_tod.py` re-tire placebo ET Null A dans le meme histogramme de `slot` de seance (Null B, permutation des signes sur les MEMES barres, etait deja apparie par construction). (2) **Les noms de signaux ne sont pas des mecanismes**: `T:NDB_10`, `ed171c5755ae` et `fea1f3e0a589` ont n=4155, t=3.813171 et Ryr=61.162887 IDENTIQUES a la 6e decimale — le meme mecanisme arrive par trois corpus. Deduplication sur l'EMPREINTE de comportement (vecteur (n,t) sur les 9 brackets), cohorte ET denominateur. (3) **~35-43 specs Pine par actif ont echoue de facon TRANSITOIRE** sous 6 processus concurrents (pression memoire, 5 GB de pointe sur NAS100) et le `except Exception` presentait ca comme une incompatibilite du corpus: les 32 manquants d'US500 se reconstruisent **32/32 sans erreur** machine libre. La passe TOD couvre 352/352, la passe de controle seulement ~310. | ⭐ **LE DEFAUT PRINCIPAL, ET C'EST LUI QUI RENVERSE LA LECTURE: la cohorte reelle est DEJA PRESELECTIONNEE sur t>2 dans le sweep d'origine, sur CES MEMES donnees; le placebo est tire frais.** Comparer leurs taux de survie bruts revient a comparer des candidats filtres a des candidats non filtres, et la batterie ne fait alors que re-mesurer le filtre. Mesure: **21.8 % des cellules reelles atteignent t>2 sur >=1 des 9 brackets contre 2.9 % des placebos** — c'est cet ecart, pas un edge, qui produit le rapport brut. Le seul rapport interpretable conditionne les DEUX cotes au meme crible observable. | **RESULTAT, passe TOD, dedupliquee, conditionnelle a preselection egale: reel 185/459 = 40.3 % contre placebo 37/124 = 29.8 %, soit 1.35x (Fisher p=0.021).** Brut et sans conditionnement il valait 11.33x (p=2e-53) — a ne jamais citer. **DECOMPOSITION PAR ACTIF, et elle est le resultat: NAS100 SEUL 124/228 = 54.4 % contre 7/46 = 15.2 %, soit 3.57x (p<0.0001); LES CINQ AUTRES 61/231 = 26.4 % contre 30/78 = 38.5 %, soit 0.69x (p=0.98) — le placebo y survit DAVANTAGE que le reel.** Par actif: GER40 0.79x, US30 1.20x (n=13, tout passe des deux cotes), US500 2/60 contre 0/9, FRA40 et XAUUSD quasi vides. | ⚠️ **LE CONTROLE A PLACEBO UNIFORME NE CONFIRME PAS le conditionnel: a perimetre identique (1 880 cellules) TOD donne 1.29x p=0.053 et UNIF 1.15x p=0.217 — aucun des deux n'est significatif a 5 %.** Le 1.35x/1.49x de la passe TOD complete tient donc a sa couverture superieure autant qu'a sa methode. **Et il reste une asymetrie residuelle en faveur du reel** (la cohorte reelle porte une selection ANTERIEURE sur les memes donnees que le placebo n'a pas), donc **1.35x est une borne HAUTE, pas une estimation.** | ⭐ **LE SEUL CRITERE QUE LE HASARD NE FRANCHIT PAS EST LA REPLICATION CROSS-ACTIFS: 18 mecanismes reels passent sur >=2 actifs contre 0/117 placebos (Fisher p=0.0026); sur >=3 actifs, 1 contre 0 (p=0.72, non significatif).** C'est la meme conclusion que toute la campagne: ce qui discrimine n'est pas d'empiler des tests sur un actif, c'est d'exiger le meme mecanisme sur plusieurs marches. | **ET LES 18 SONT UNE SEULE FAMILLE, DEJA CONNUE: cassure/tendance sur NAS100 et GER40.** Lecture des sources: `7f6d1ec4e1fc` = cassure du plus-haut 126 barres + EMA21>EMA50 + filtre RSI; `381cce4812bf` = canal de Donchian 20; `1a3ba1745877` = nuage Ichimoku; `T:LRforecast_20/60` = prevision par regression lineaire; `T:Dunnigan_thrust`, `T:Keltner_break_2.5` = poussee/cassure de canal. **11 des 18 impliquent NAS100, 12 impliquent GER40** — c'est la famille de la brique 1 et de l'ORB GER40 de l'etagere, pas un mecanisme neuf. Le seul a repliquer sur 4 actifs (`c8bfc91ba728`, composite Reversal123 + Bill Williams AC) est petit: n median 153, +4.5 R/an, RoMaD 0.58. | **REPONSE A LA QUESTION POSEE: non, empiler six tests ne permet pas de discerner la chance d'un edge dans cette cohorte — conditionnellement au crible, reel et placebo sont indiscernables partout SAUF sur NAS100.** La campagne re-derive ses propres conclusions par un chemin independant: NAS100 (et accessoirement GER40) portent tout, l'or et le CAC ne portent rien, et le mecanisme survivant est la cassure de tendance deja au livre. **Aucun candidat neuf, aucune promotion.** `scratchpad/battery_full.py`, `battery_tod.py`, `battery_verdict.py`, `_inx/battery_tod_*.csv`, `_inx/battery_full_*.csv`, `_inx/battery_verdict_dedup.csv`.

La regle teste `k1 < 50`: la meme barre etait donc un SHORT au backtest et RIEN en live. **Cause racine: `pd.Series.rolling(n).mean()` maintient une somme GLISSANTE (ajoute l'entrant, retranche le sortant), donc son erreur flottante depend de TOUTES les donnees amont — la valeur n'est pas une fonction pure des n dernieres barres.** Correctif: `_sma` recalcule une somme EXPLICITE par fenetre, a ordre de sommation fixe, donc window-local et deterministe. **Et c'est aussi la bonne reponse mathematiquement**: la vraie valeur est 50, et `50 < 50` est faux — le short du backtest etait l'erreur d'arrondi, pas le silence du driver. L'ecart de **6.47 R/an** est le cout FTMO, quasi tout en swap crypto — le meme 6.24 qui separe 47.38 de 40.90 sur le livre actuel. **(3) 46.97** = la meme chose que 46.81, mais sommee sleeve par sleeve **sur le span propre de chacune** au lieu du calendrier commun: artefact de presentation de 0.16, sans portee. | **TABLE COMPLETE, brut contre net: AGRESSIF (KAER@0.5R) brut 47.38 R/an maxDD 17.05 RoMaD 2.78 -> 13.89 %/an; NET 40.90 / 17.36 / 2.36 -> 11.78 %/an. KAER->CAND@0.5R brut 53.28 / 15.29 / 3.49 -> 17.43 %/an; NET 46.81 / 17.16 / 2.73 -> 13.63 %/an.** | ⚠️ **CE QUE L'ECHANGE APPORTE REELLEMENT: R/an +5.90 (identique brut et net, les trois sleeves NAS100 etant intraday donc a swap nul), mais maxDD -1.76 BRUT contre seulement **-0.20 NET**, RoMaD +0.71 brut contre **+0.37 net**, et %/an a risque egal +3.53 pt brut contre **+1.86 pt net**.** Le maxDD est path-dependent: le drag du swap est quasi continu (la crypto tient ~20 nuits par trade), il creuse les drawdowns des DEUX livres et fait converger leurs pires episodes, donc l'avantage de chemin de HMASTO s'evapore largement une fois les frais payes. **Idem pour la ruine funded a 0.5 %/trade: 10.0 % -> 5.9 % NET, et non 7.3 % -> 3.8 % comme annonce sur les series brutes.** | **CE QUI N'EST PAS AFFECTE: la reponse sur le challenge (P(valider) 88.2 % a 1 %/trade, mediane 2.8 mois) etait DEJA calculee sur la serie NETTE — elle tient telle quelle. Comparer deux livres en BRUT est valide pour un DELTA de R/an entre sleeves sans nuit, mais pas pour un delta de maxDD, de RoMaD, de %/an a risque egal ni de ruine — toutes ces metriques sont non-lineaires et le drag les deplace inegalement.** `scratchpad/nas_cand_ftmo.py`, `scratchpad/ftmo_swaps.py`.
